## 2026-10-25 — Step 5: course audio
- Owner: the dialogue works; go ahead with the audio; a main woman's voice with Orus for balance. Chosen: Sulafat ("warm") with Orus as narrators; Charon moves to the dialogue voices. Recorded in `docs/DASHBOARD_PLAN.md` and `docs/BOOKS.md` ("Course audio").
- What is spoken: every example sentence, dialogue line and reading paragraph of the 30 books: 4,153 items, 4,119 distinct files, about 373,000 characters (about 7.5 hours; about $6 with Flash at Google's price until 31 Dec 2026). 197 dialogue speakers: names and roles mapped to women's or men's voices (`site/src/data/books/audio.js`), unknown roles take the contrasting gender; a name keeps its voice across a book. A2 examples are slower.
- `/data/audio/<book>.json` (site build): the plan with a hash per file. `/api/speech?action=generate` (admin): makes the next missing files of a book for about 40 seconds (three at a time), encodes MP3 at 40 kbps (`@breezystack/lamejs`, new dependency), uploads to the public Storage bucket `course-audio` (created by the function if missing) and updates `<book>/manifest.json`. `maxDuration` 60 s for `api/speech.js`.
- `/app/audio` (admin only, not linked): progress per book, minutes and cost so far, Start/Pause; keeps calling the generator book after book while the tab is open; a book that fails three times in a row is skipped and its error shown.
- Reader: play button beside each example, Listen on dialogues (lines appear and are marked as they are spoken) and readings (paragraph by paragraph), only where the files exist; audio stops on page turns and when leaving the book. Two keys in nine languages.
- Checked: unit tests 61/61 (MP3 encoding, speech fallbacks), the site build makes 30 plans, the reader with a mocked plan, manifest and files (examples, dialogue, reading play and highlight, no errors), German B1 unit 1 and a practice round play through.
- Owner to do: open `/app/audio` signed in as admin and press Start; keep the tab open until every book is done.
- Rate limit: Google's Tier 1 allows 10 requests a minute for `gemini-3.8-flash-tts`; the first run sent three at a time and got 429s. The generator now sends one request every 6.7 s (`TTS_PER_MINUTE`, default 9; raise it in Vercel after a tier upgrade), stops a call at the first 429 without trying the fallback, and returns `retryAfter`; `/app/audio` waits that long and carries on instead of counting a failure. At this pace the 4,142 files take about 7 to 8 hours with the tab open. Unit test for the 429 path (62/62).
- Daily limit: generation stopped at 72 files; most likely Google's Tier 1 daily limit for the TTS model (a forum report says 100 requests a day; Google no longer lists it). Owner chose option A: fewer, bigger requests. `/api/speech?action=generate` now groups the missing sentences of one block that share voice and style (at most 8 and 2,500 characters; about 1,045 requests for the 4,142 files instead of 4,142), asks for a long pause after each sentence, and cuts the recording at the N-1 longest silences, keeping the cut only when those pauses are clearly longer than any other (1.4 times) and each piece's length fits its text; otherwise the group is redone one sentence per request. Calls run up to about 75 s (`maxDuration` 120). `/app/audio` shows recordings made per request. Unit tests for the cutting and grouping (65/65). Not yet tried against Google's real voices: check the first books by listening.

## 2026-10-26 — Course audio limit checked; AI tutor vision recorded
- Course audio (`/app/audio`): Google's Gemini API allows 100 text-to-speech requests a day for `gemini-3.8-flash-tts` on Tier 1. The page does not recognise the daily limit (it reads "retry in 22h…" as 60 seconds, caps waits at two minutes, and asks again every minute), and a group whose pauses cannot be cut is redone one sentence at a time, spending up to eight of the day's requests. 217 of 5,455 files done. Options given to the owner: fix the page (recognise the daily limit, resume at the reset, redo groups in halves), or move generation to Google Cloud Text-to-Speech (same Gemini voices, about 150 requests a minute, set-up by the owner). Waiting for the owner's answer; nothing changed.
- AI tutor: the owner's vision recorded in `docs/DASHBOARD_PLAN.md` ("AI tutor") for its own planning session.

New `?action=live-board { session, calls }`: each call checked like the written tutor's replies (`cleanBoard`, `cleanExercise` after turning list answers into the engine's shape, `cleanSentence`), stored as a tutor message, the session board updated; a refused call comes back with a reason for the tutor; unknown tools refused. `?action=live-answer { session, answer }`: the exercise results stored as the student's turn and returned as the line to tell the tutor (the results logic now shared with the written turn). `?action=pronounce` with `live: true`: the open sentence is the latest `say_this` (transcript lines after it do not close it), scored and stored, `{ tell }` returned, no written reply and no charge (the call's ticks count the time). None of the three charges time.
- B, dashboard: the call's page is the board (left, the written tutor's board and its writing-in animation, shared through `window.AlimunTutor.kit`) beside the tutor's face and the captions (on a phone the face first). Tool calls from Google go to `live-board`, the board updates and Google gets each result by id; exercises are played by the shared engine and their results go to `live-answer`, then to the tutor as the student's turn; the pronunciation card records from the call's own microphone stream (the tutor does not hear the sentence meanwhile), scores it through `pronounce` live, shows the marked words and scores, and tells the tutor; "Hear it slowly" asks the tutor to say it slowly. The written tutor can also spell on the board. Past transcripts skip board-only rows. One new key (`app_tl_recording`) in nine languages.
- C: the brief (written tutor and call) includes the latest class notes the teacher published for one of the student's active classes in the last 14 days (grammar focus, words, exercises, homework and due date, the teacher's note) and the latest completed 1:1 note; the call offers early to go over that lesson. ## 2026-10-26 — AI tutor, part 7: published to students, silence hang-up, uncounted time charged
- Owner: "yes" to the Privacy and Terms sentences for the call; asked whether to count only active talking minutes and whether idle time costs money. Answer given: idle time costs Google about $0.005 a minute while the microphone streams (speaking and re-reading the conversation only happen on turns); counting only talking time would rest on the browser's word and let silent calls grow; recommended keeping "10 minutes of call time" with an automatic hang-up after silence.
- Privacy (`site/src/blocks/legal/gdpr.html` and the seven translations): after the sentence on Microsoft Azure, the live call (voice, conversation and the brief sent to Google's Gemini API, which may process them outside the EU; audio not stored; transcript kept like other tutor conversations), with its `[COPY NEEDED: legal review ...]`. Terms: "What you say to the tutor is used only to hold the conversation, turn your speech into text or score your pronunciation, and recordings are not stored." Both hashes re-approved in `site/locked.json`; `npm run build:site` passes (locked blocks check included); only the sources are committed. `docs/AI_TUTOR_LEGAL_LIVE.md` removed now that it is published. Then `app_settings` `tutor_live` set to `all` through the connector: every student with time can call.
- Silence: `js/app/tutor-live.js` hangs up after 2 minutes with nobody speaking (5 with an exercise, the sentence card or a recording open), with "Are you still there?" and "I'm still here" 15 seconds before; the tutor speaking, the transcripts, board calls and the student's clicks and typing on the board count as activity. 3 `app_tl_*` keys in nine languages.
- Time: found while answering the owner, a browser that stopped ticking and then ended or abandoned a call was charged at most 90 seconds. `api/tutor.js` now charges a live call's uncounted time up to the end of its pass (at most 10 minutes) whenever it is ended, reopened, or closed as idle by the next session or turn (`chargeUnticked`, `closeStale`).
- Checked: `npm run build`, `npm run build:site`; every `api/` file and the inline scripts pass `node --check`; the server against the in-memory stand-ins (66 of 66: ending after five minutes without ticks charges them, an abandoned call pays its whole pass when the next starts, a normal end charges only the seconds since the last tick; pronunciation 50 of 50); in Chromium against the stand-in of Google's live protocol, the warning about 105 seconds after the last speech, "I'm still here" hiding it, the hang-up 2 minutes later with the end screen and one `end` call; the sample call in English on desktop and Arabic on a phone unchanged, no console errors besides the local-only level test file.

## 2026-10-26 — AI tutor, part 6: paid time
- Owner: "build part 6". Built as in `docs/AI_TUTOR_PLAN.md` part 6 and the answers in section 10a (question 4: packs of 30 and 120 minutes, prices set in Stripe by the owner, bought minutes expire after 12 months, free-plan students may buy, the offer only when the time is used up, inside the Tutor).
- Database (`supabase/migrations/202610271400_tutor_paid_time.sql`): bought minutes need an expiry, which one balance column cannot hold, so the bought-or-added bucket is now made of lots (`tutor_credit_lots`: what was added, what is left, when a purchase expires; students read their own, nothing written from the browser). `tutor_time.credit_seconds` always equals the sum of the lots. Using the tutor takes the lot that expires first (time an admin added and refunds never expire, so they go last); an admin removing time takes time that never expires first. `tutor_refresh` expires what is left of a purchase 12 months after it (ledger `expire` on the credit bucket). `tutor_add_credit` writes a purchase as a lot expiring in 12 months and checks the Stripe checkout id after locking the balance, so two deliveries add once; a purchase without a checkout id is refused. Same function names and arguments, so the server works before and after the file runs. Production checked first through the connector: its four time functions match `202610262000_tutor_time.sql`, one balance row, no bought time. Applied on the owner's "yes, apply the migration" through the connector, one statement at a time (the policy created directly, the table being new). Verify: row security on, one policy, every balance equal to its lots, the four functions closed to the browser, `anon` cannot read the lots and students cannot write them, one `tutor_add_credit`, and the four function bodies identical to the ones tested locally.
- Server: `api/_lib/tutor-packs.js` (the two packs, `STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_30` and `STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_120`; minutes from the Stripe price's metadata `minutes`, else 30 and 120). `api/stripe.js`: the packs are one-time prices in the catalogue (type `tutor_time`); checkout of a pack ignores the browser's return addresses and comes back to `/app?checkout=tutor&session_id={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}#/tutor` (cancel: `#/tutor`); a student with a plan may buy packs; `?action=prices` lists `tutor30` and `tutor120` with amount and minutes. `api/webhooks/stripe.js`: `case 'tutor_time'` reads the paid line items from Stripe (not the checkout's metadata), adds the minutes through `tutor_add_credit` with the checkout id, and fails (Stripe retries) when no pack is in the checkout. `api/tutor.js` `?action=status` adds `expiring` (the soonest bought minutes and their date) and `plans` (each paid plan's monthly minutes).
- Dashboard: `js/app/tutor.js`: with no time left, the Tutor page opens on "Your tutor time is used up": when the plan's minutes come back (on a plan), or the plans with their monthly minutes in a sentence, then the packs at Stripe's prices ("30 minutes for €…") and "See the plans"; the same offer closes a written session or a call (`js/app/tutor-live.js`) that ran out; never shown while time is left (no upgrade pop-up). The time card says until when bought minutes last. `js/app/plans.js` `tutorPacks()` and `buyTutor()`. Back from Stripe (`js/app/shell.js`): "Thank you for your purchase", then the student's own ledger is checked every 3 seconds for the checkout's row (`js/app/data.js` `tutorPurchase`) and the Tutor redrawn with "We added 30 minutes to your tutor time". Admin student file: bought minutes expiring are named. 11 `app_tu_*` keys in nine languages; `.tu-out` in `css/app.css`.
- Checked: `npm run build`; every `api/` and `js/app/` file and the 423 inline scripts pass `node --check`; unit tests 84 of 84, including the new `tests/unit/tutor-paid.test.js` (8, stand-ins for Stripe and Supabase: checkout of a pack with the server's return addresses, a plan student buying, unknown prices refused, the packs' prices and minutes from metadata, the webhook adding the price's minutes and not the metadata's, once over a repeated event and over `async_payment_succeeded`, nothing before payment, a checkout without a pack failing, the status's expiry and plan minutes). The migration against a local Postgres 16 with the part 1 migration under it, run twice (24 of 24: a balance from before becoming a lot that never expires, buying, the repeated checkout id, the 12-month date, refusing a purchase without id, free minutes first then the lot expiring first with the refund kept, expiry once with its ledger row, an admin removing time never-expiring first and never below zero, the ledger adding up, plan minutes before bought ones, the browser unable to run the functions or write lots, a student seeing only their own lots). In Chromium (sample, prices stood in): the out-of-time screen in English on desktop and Arabic on a phone, the sample charging nothing, the return note; no offer while time is left; no console errors besides the local-only level test file; nothing wider than the phone.
- Owner's steps in Stripe (live mode): Product catalogue, add a product "Tutor time, 30 minutes" with a one-time price in EUR (add the USD, CAD, AUD and MAD currency options like the other prices if wanted) and, under the price's metadata, `minutes` = `30`; the same for "Tutor time, 120 minutes" with `minutes` = `120`. Copy the two price ids (`price_...`) into Vercel as `STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_30` and `STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_120` and redeploy. The webhook needs no change (it already receives `checkout.session.completed` and `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`). Until the two variables exist, the out-of-time screen shows the plans only.
- Not checked from here: a real payment, Stripe's live webhook adding the minutes, the price amounts the owner chooses. A refund in Stripe does not remove bought minutes by itself (as for private classes): the admin removes them on the student's file.
- Pushed to main on 26 Oct 2026 as c126a95 (from a deploy session, since the build session could not push): build regenerated nothing, node --check, the inline-script check and 84 unit tests passed; live on alimun.com (js/app/plans.js has buyTutor, /app/ loads) and scripts/ci/smoke-prod.sh passed. Migration not re-applied (public.tutor_credit_lots confirmed in production). Still waiting on the owner: the two Stripe prices and STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_30 / STRIPE_PRICE_TUTOR_120 in Vercel.

## 2026-10-27 — AI tutor, live call: turn-taking, honest pronunciation, speaking at the level
- Owner: the tutor stops mid-sentence or changes topic after one word, does not correct pronunciation properly when asked, and is not set to the student's level. The owner's Spanish A2 calls of 27 Oct showed it: noise heard as "¿Dónde", replies after a single word, "Buenas tíos a todas" praised as "a great job" (the say_this card was on the board but the tutor asked the student to repeat in the call, so Azure never scored it), and "español profesional" at A2 until the student asked for English.
- `api/_lib/tutor-live.js`: Google's voice activity detection set in the pass (`realtimeInputConfig`, `activityFor`): low start and end sensitivity, 200 ms of speech before a turn starts, and the tutor waits 1.5 s of silence at A1 and A2, 1.2 s at B1, 0.9 s otherwise (Google's default answers sooner). If Google refuses these settings, the pass is asked for again with its defaults (logged). Instructions: `levelRules` per level (A1 and A2: everyday words, sentences of 5 to 8 words, no professional or idiomatic language, slow, the meaning in the interface language when not understood, grammar in that language; the level decides the difficulty, the brief's goals only the topics); taking turns (a word or half a sentence: wait or ask, never guess or change the subject; maybe noise); pronunciation honest (never "great" when a word was wrong), corrected after every turn for the rest of the call once the student asks, and checked on the card with say_this (the student taps record; feedback only from the score). `api/tutor.js` passes the session's level to the pass.
- Checked: `npm run build`, `node --check`, the inline-script check, unit tests 89 of 89 (new `tests/unit/tutor-live-setup.test.js`). Not checked from here: a real call (no Google key in this session); the owner tries a call after the deploy.

## 2026-10-27 — AI tutor, live call: words the student never said (the phone touched or moved)
- Owner: the call still hears words they are not saying, a lot, especially when the phone moves or is touched. Cause: every sound of the microphone went to Google, whose transcriber turned handling noise into words (the voice activity settings of the morning only decide when the tutor answers); and the 48 kHz microphone was taken down to 16 kHz by keeping every third sample, so rubbing and rustling above 8 kHz folded into the voice range.
- `js/app/tutor-live.js`: the microphone now goes through a 120 Hz high-pass (handling rumble) and a 7 kHz low-pass (nothing folds down), then `speechGate`: 20 ms frames; it opens on 120 ms of sound clearly above the room's learned noise, at least half of it voiced (few zero crossings: a tap is too short, rubbing and rustling are hiss), keeps the 240 ms before so first syllables and an initial "s" stay, and closes after 700 ms without speech; what it does not let through is sent as silence. While the tutor is speaking it needs a voice 2.5 times louder for 200 ms, so its own sound in the room does not cut it off. About 240 ms more delay on the student's side. The pronunciation card still records the microphone as it is.
- Checked: new `tests/unit/tutor-live-gate.test.js` (6: silence and taps send nothing, rustling sends nothing, a vowel gets through from its start, a word starting with a hiss keeps it, quiet sound ignored and a clear voice kept while the tutor speaks, a short "sí" gets through); unit tests 95 of 95, `npm run build`, `node --check`, inline scripts. Not checked from here: a real phone call (owner to try).

## 2026-10-27 — Course books: German A1, Spanish A1 to B1, English C1 (25 books)
- Owner: "do german a1, spanish a1 a2 b1 english c1 etc". Written: five course books (German A1, Spanish A1, A2, B1, English C1; eight units each, four lessons and a unit test per unit) and their four goal books each (moving abroad, work, study and exams, travel; six units each): 25 books, 160 units, drafts for native-teacher review. Spanish books teach the Spanish of Spain and say where Latin America differs. Every book passes `scripts/check-book.mjs`. Writers' doubts in `docs/BOOK_REVIEW_NOTES.md`.
- `site/src/data/books/audio.js`: Spanish roles (Camarero, Taquillera, Doctora and others) and Señor/Señora get matching voices; the audio plans of the existing 30 books are unchanged (checked by hash).
- Explanations translated by helper agents: Spanish, French, German and Portuguese for all 25 books; Italian 24 (English C1 travel missing); Chinese 13 (the five course books and some goal books); Arabic 6 (English C1 and most goal books missing). Missing translations show in English. Each file checked for missing strings, markers and gaps.
- Owner, on accents: forgiving up to B1, then half right. `js/app/exercises.js`: in Spanish exercises up to B1 a typed answer without written accents (á é í ó ú ü; ñ is a letter) counts as right and the feedback shows the accented form ("Watch the accents:"); from B2 it counts as wrong with "Almost! Check the accents." (true half points would need the scores to take fractions; no Spanish B2 book exists yet). `reader.js` passes the book level. Two `app_ex_accent_*` keys in nine languages; `tests/unit/accents.test.js`.
- Not done: audio for the 25 books (from `/app/audio`), the 32 missing translations, native review.
- Cost: the writing and translating ran as many parallel agents and used a large share of the owner's weekly limit; the owner was not told the size first. Next time: give the size and an estimate before starting work of this scale.
- Checked: `npm run build`, `npm run build:site` (earlier in the session), unit tests 99 of 99, `node --check`.
- Course audio for the 25 new books made by the owner from `/app/audio` (new spending limit, default $12, `js/app/audio-admin.js`): every file present, 373 minutes, about $11.19 at $0.03 a minute (estimate was $10.40).

## 2026-11-06 — Forgot password and teacher invites fixed

Owner: "forgot password, creation of teacher from admin panel and more are broken". Cause (Supabase auth logs): every Supabase auth email link went through /auth/v1/verify and was sent on to the project's Site URL, `http://localhost:3000`, because https://alimun.com is not on the Auth redirect allow-list (every auth request today logged `referer: http://localhost:3000`, even signups sent with `redirect_to=https://alimun.com/signup.html`). The invite itself worked (account, teacher role and approved teacher profile were created; the link signed the teacher in), but the browser landed on localhost. The same applied to reset emails, which also came from Supabase's built-in mailer.
- New `api/auth.js?action=reset`: builds the link from the hashed token straight to `alimun.com/<lang>/reset-password.html?token_hash=…&type=recovery` and sends it through Resend. Same answer whether or not the account exists; per-instance limits per IP and per email. If Resend is missing or refuses, it answers 503 and `forgot-password.html` falls back to Supabase's own mailer.
- Admin invite (`api/admin/teacher.js?action=invite`): the link is now `alimun.com/reset-password.html?token_hash=…&type=invite`; the invited person chooses a password, then goes to /app/teach (teachers) or welcome.html (students). Email copy updated; ink header band removed (no black backgrounds).
- `reset-password.html`: accepts `token_hash` links and only uses the token when the password is saved (mail scanners opening the link do not use it up); the old PASSWORD_RECOVERY links still work.
- Checked locally: forgot page posts to the API (de page sends `lang: de`), a bad token shows "link expired", no console errors; API validation paths.
- For the owner (Supabase dashboard, not reachable from the connector): Authentication → URL Configuration: Site URL `https://alimun.com`; Redirect URLs `https://alimun.com/**` and `https://www.alimun.com/**`. Authentication → Emails → SMTP: Resend, so Supabase's own emails are not limited to a few per hour. Live check after deploy: `/api/auth?action=reset` answers 503 `email_unavailable` for an address with no account, so `RESEND_API_KEY` is not set in Vercel production: resets fall back to the Supabase mailer (still landing on localhost until the Site URL is fixed) and invites show the admin the link to copy, which now works. Smoke test 8 of 8.
- Follow-up: owner set the Supabase Site URL and Redirect URLs (auth logs now show `https://alimun.com/` instead of localhost). Vercel's Redeploy button asked for an emailed verification code that did not arrive, so this push to `main` starts the new production deployment that picks up the environment variables.
- Vercel check (GitHub commit statuses): two Vercel projects are linked to this repo. `youssefbouchtaoui-4103s-projects/alimun-site` serves alimun.com and built every `main` commit since 20 Oct successfully (latest live: a0d40b5); only four intermediate draft commits on 1 Nov failed there, all superseded by later successful deploys. `abdul-edilizia/alimun-site` has failed on every commit (first "build-rate-limit, upgrade to Pro", then build failures; likely the Hobby limit of 12 functions, the repo now has 15). Recommended to the owner: disconnect or delete the `abdul-edilizia` project. `vercel.json` now skips builds of `claude/*` and `ccr-*` session branches, which doubled every push.

## 2026-11-07 — Growth step 3: activity

Owner: "settings is enough, start step 3" (no one-time country question for existing users).
- Database (`supabase/migrations/202611071200_activity.sql`, applied statement by statement): `activity_events` holds only what no other table records: `open_dashboard` (once per tab), `view` (section opened, the same one again after 10 minutes) written by the dashboards, and `subscribed`, `purchased`, `payment_failed`, `subscription_ended` written by the Stripe webhook. Browsers may insert only their own `open_dashboard`/`view` rows, at most 120 per 10 minutes (extra rows dropped quietly); only admins read. `activity_all()` combines these with sign-up and last sign-in (auth.users), exercises, practice, flashcards (one line per day), tutor sessions, homework, attendance, classes joined and left, 1:1s, waiting lists, messages sent (no text) and news choices. `admin_activity_timeline(user)` and `admin_activity_overview()` refuse non-admins. Events older than 24 months are deleted daily (pg_cron `alimun-activity-purge`, 03:27 UTC). Tested as the real teacher and admin in rolled-back transactions: own rows accepted, others' rows, server-only events and oversized details refused, rate limit keeps 120 of 130, server writes unlimited, non-admins cannot read or call the admin functions.
- Found while testing: `is_client_write()` answers false inside security definer functions (it looks at current_user); the rate limit uses `auth.role()` instead. Worth knowing for other triggers.
- Dashboards (`js/app/data.js` `AlimunData.track`): student and teacher dashboards record opening and sections; nothing is sent signed out, in samples, or when an admin views someone's dashboard (writes are read-only there).
- Admin: new Activity page (`js/app/admin-activity.js`, menu under People): active today, 7 and 30 days, a 30-day bar chart, new accounts, where students stop (account, dashboard, level test, did something, in a class, paying), people slipping away (nothing for 14 to 90 days, linked to their file) and countries. Each student's and teacher's file has a Timeline card, newest first, with "Show older".
- Real numbers today: 23 people, 15 student accounts, 13 took the level test, 4 did something beyond that, 2 in a class, 1 paying; 5 active in the last 7 days.
- Checked: Activity page and timeline in sample mode on desktop and phone, student and teacher dashboards load with no errors and send nothing signed out; `npm run build` passes.
- For the owner: the Privacy page should say that Alimun records what people do in their dashboards to run and improve the service and keeps it 24 months; draft wording to approve before it changes.

## 2026-11-09 — AI tutor, live call: no more words the student never said, and pronunciation checked on every turn
- Owner: "when i am not talking, it is like he listens to words i have never said ... the azure pronunciation thingy is not working, because clearly he doesn't correct me when i make mistakes". The 8 Nov Spanish A2 call showed both: lines such as "Hola. Hola." and "Anda con una nariz de chontaduro" saved as the student's, and "les libros" (for "lees libros") answered with "¡Exacto!". `ai_usage` had no `tutor_pron` row in that call: Azure works (two scores on 4 Nov), but it only ran when the tutor put a say_this card on the board, which it never did.
- Cause of the phantom words: between turns the browser kept streaming the gated microphone (silence, and whatever noise the gate let through) to Google, whose transcriber turned it into words and started turns on them.
- `api/_lib/tutor-live.js`: the pass now switches Google's own voice detection off (`automaticActivityDetection.disabled`) and tells the browser `turn: { manual, silence_ms }`. If Google refuses that, the calmer Google detection as before, then its defaults (logged).
- `js/app/tutor-live.js` `turnTaker`: a turn starts only after 240 ms of voiced sound has passed the gate (320 ms while the tutor speaks or for half a second after); a cough, a "hm", a tap or a short noise is dropped. The browser sends `activityStart`, the audio, then `activityEnd` after the level's silence (1.5 s at A1/A2, 1.2 s at B1, 0.9 s above), and sends nothing at all between turns.
- Pronunciation on every turn: the turn's audio (at most 30 s) and Google's transcript of it go to the new `?action=live-pron` (its own limit of 30 a minute). Azure scores it against the transcript and the recording is dropped. When a word scored under 60 (the two worst, with the sound that was off), the tutor gets a `<school>` note between turns (`clientContent`, `turnComplete: false`, so it never cuts the tutor off) and corrects it at its next turn: at most one word every few turns at A1/A2, every turn once the student asks. No note when the turn does not match its transcript (accuracy under 40 or completeness under 60: another language or noise). Cost in `ai_usage` as `tutor_pron`, about $1.30 an hour of the student's speech.
- Instructions: a word that is not the word needed ("les" for "lees", "pero" for "perro") is wrong: "almost", the right word slowly, repeat; never "exactly". The speech checker is exact and the tutor's own hearing is not; a word it marked twice goes on the say_this card.
- Limit: the check scores how each word was said against what Google heard. A word mispronounced into another real word ("les") is caught by the tutor's rule, not by Azure.
- Checked: new `tests/unit/tutor-live-turns.test.js` (10) and the fallback tests in `tutor-live-setup.test.js`; all 112 unit tests, `npm run build`, `node --check`, the sample call in a browser. Not checked from here: a real call (no Google key in this session). The owner tries a call after the deploy.

## 2026-11-10 — AI tutor, live call: measuring the real cost, and keeping long calls from costing more each minute
- Owner: asked how to lower the tutor's cost without losing quality; "go ahead with 1 and 2" (measure, cap the context).
- Spend so far (ai_usage, 26 Oct to 8 Nov): live calls $0.83 of $0.87, 17 calls, about 24 minutes. That figure is the flat estimate of $0.035 a minute (`LIVE_USD_PER_SEC`), not Google's bill.
- Measuring (`supabase/migrations/202611101000_tutor_live_usage.sql`, applied through the connector and verified): `tutor_sessions.live_usage` jsonb. The browser keeps each `usageMetadata` Google sends in the call (prompt, cached, response, thoughts and tool tokens, audio and text apart, connection and second) and sends them with the next tick; `live.cleanUsage` keeps whole numbers only, 100 a tick, 400 a session. Measurement only: the student's time and the budget still use the ticks and the estimate. Google's docs do not say whether a report covers one turn or the session so far; to be read once real calls are in.
- Context: `contextWindowCompression` now `triggerTokens: 24000`, `slidingWindow.targetTokens: 12000` (Google's default waits for 80% of the model's window). Google drops the oldest turns, it does not summarise them; the instructions and the brief always stay. Only calls of about 10 minutes and more are touched (average call so far: 85 seconds). If Google refuses the sizes, its own sliding window as before (logged).
- Owner's idea, a cheaper live model for the free and cheapest tiers: not built. Google's price list (10 Nov) charges the same for audio on every live model ($3.00 per million tokens in, $12.00 out, i.e. $0.005 and $0.018 a minute): Gemini 3.8 Live, 3.1 Flash Live Preview and 2.5 Flash Native Audio. The older ones are cheaper only for text, a small part of a call, so they would save almost nothing and sound worse. The Gemini API free tier is not usable for students (Google may use that data to improve its products). Told the owner; waiting on their choice.
- Checked: 3 new tests in `tests/unit/tutor-live-setup.test.js`; 115 of 115 unit tests, `npm run build`, `node --check`, the sample call in a browser. Not checked from here: a real call (no Google key in this session).

## 2026-11-10 — Homepage videos slow on phones
- Owner: "when on mobile and open alimuns homepage, videos take too long to load. that is unacceptable."
- Cause (measured on an iPhone profile against alimun.com): 6.6 MB was fetched before any scroll. The languages section far down the page started its first city clip (London, 3.5 MB) the moment the page loaded; the old hero's hidden video wall downloaded the hero clip a second time; the six city posters (about 740 KB) loaded eagerly; and every file under `/videos` and `/images` was served with `max-age=0`, so nothing was kept between visits.
- Fixes: the languages section (`site/src/blocks/home/languages.html`) now waits until it is about a screen away before setting its posters (`data-poster`, written by `site/scripts/localize.mjs`) and playing; `localize.mjs` drops the sources and autoplay of the hidden old-hero video (`withoutHiddenHeroVideo`), leaving the locked `02-hero.html` untouched; `vercel.json` caches `/videos`, `/images` and `/fonts` for 7 days (stale-while-revalidate 30 days); the six city clips were re-encoded at the same size and names (London 3.5 to 1.7 MB, Sintra 3.8 to 1.7, Rome 3.6 to 1.7, Barcelona 1.6 to 1.1, Berlin 1.5 to 0.9, Paris 1.0 to 0.6; WebM copies smaller too), frames compared side by side with no visible difference.
- Result (local build, iPhone 13 profile): 1.16 MB on load, the only video being the hero clip; the city clips and product films still play as you scroll, phone and desktop, no script errors.
- Not changed: the product films (already loaded only when shown) and the hero clip itself (0.9 MB, in a locked block).
## 2026-11-10 — AI tutor, live call: the real cost of a call, a cheaper way to keep long calls, and the owner's test call
- The owner's test call (English A1, Sofia, 10 minutes, 48 turns): Google reported 56 usage figures, one per turn (the response count varies by turn, the prompt grows: each turn re-reads the whole conversation). Priced with Google's list: listening audio 336,136 tokens $1.01, text in 176,424 tokens $0.13, spoken answers 6,975 tokens $0.08, thoughts and tool calls about $0.02; **$1.25 for 10 minutes**, plus Azure $0.07 (49 checks) and summary under $0.01. Our flat estimate said $0.35; Google's per-minute figures ($0.005 listening, $0.018 speaking) do not include the re-reading. Nothing was cached (cachedContentTokenCount 0). The 24,000-token cap of earlier today never triggered (the conversation reached 17,712).
- Fresh connections (`js/app/tutor-live.js` `rotate`): once the conversation has grown by 5,500 tokens since the connection started, the call moves to a new pass between turns (after the tutor's answer, not while an exercise, the sentence card or a board call is pending). The new pass has the instructions and the last 30 lines of the call as text (`api/tutor.js` live-start, was 12), not its audio. What the student says during the switch is held and sent once Google is ready; the tutor is not prompted to speak. Simulated on the owner's call: $1.22 becomes $0.55. Tested in a browser with a fake Google connection and microphone: one switch, the student's words during it arrive on the new connection, no extra prompt, no console errors.
- Owner's findings in the same call, and the changes (`api/_lib/tutor-live.js`):
  - Said goodbye with 3 minutes left and again with 1 minute left: the tutor now knows the minutes left at the start of each pass, never wraps up on its own, and is told inside `<school>` at about 2 minutes (no new topic) and 1 minute (recap, what to practise, goodbye) (`timeNotes`).
  - Took accented words for other languages ("gut", "schlafen", "Não", "dos" in an English call): the transcriber gets language hints (`inputAudioTranscription.languageCodes`, the course language then the interface language; if Google refuses them, detection as before), and the tutor treats a near foreign word as the target word it fits, asking "Did you say good?" when unsure.
  - Asked to be taught rather than questioned, several times: new rule "teach, then involve" (a 20 to 30 second explanation with examples and the board, one check question, then practice, then their own sentences); when asked for a lesson the tutor picks the point itself instead of asking for preferences.
  - Praised wrong answers ("pen" for "sleeping" was "perfect"): an answer that is not the one asked for is wrong; the right answer is given and asked again.
- Checked: 3 new tests in `tests/unit/tutor-live-setup.test.js`; 118 of 118 unit tests, `npm run build`, `node --check`, the sample call. Not checked from here: a real call (no Google key in this session).

## 2026-11-10 — AI tutor: a cheaper spoken call to test beside the live call (admins only)
- Owner: "$1.32 is not worth it for 10 minutes ... we need to bring that down drastically" / "build a mock up before replacing what we have. i need to test it before we go ahead". The live call is unchanged for everyone; admins get a second button on the tutor page, "Try the test call".
- Cascade engine (`api/_lib/tutor-cascade.js`, `?action=cascade-turn`, admins only): the student's turn (16 kHz WAV from the call's own turn-taking) goes to Groq Whisper forced to the course language; the written tutor's model (Groq gpt-oss-120b) answers with the live call's rules (level, teach then involve, accents, honesty, time, speech checker) and the written tutor's JSON reply (`brain.replyFormat`, refactored out of `systemFor`; the written tutor's instructions are byte for byte the same); the reply is stored (student and tutor lines, board) and spoken by Azure neural text to speech on the existing Speech resource (24 kHz PCM, three sentence pieces in parallel, 12% slower at A1 and A2). The voice is chosen from the voices the resource offers (read once an hour): the tutor's gender, a multilingual voice native to the course language first, then any multilingual voice, then any voice of that language; HD voices (dearer) are not used.
- Call page (`js/app/tutor-live.js` `cascadeLink`): stands in for Google's connection, so the face and lip-sync, board, exercises, the sentence card, captions, time, idle hang-up, time notes and per-turn pronunciation checks are the same. A new turn of the student drops an answer still on its way and stops the tutor's voice. A line under the time shows the call's cost so far and how long the last answer took (`app_tl_test_cost`; admin-only text in English).
- Database (`supabase/migrations/202611101400_tutor_session_engine.sql`, applied and verified): `tutor_sessions.engine` ('cascade' for these calls). Their time is counted by ticks as usual; the live call's flat estimate is not recorded for them.
- Expected cost of the owner's 10-minute call this way: about $0.15 with per-turn pronunciation checks ($0.07 of it), about $0.08 without. Answers should take about 1.5 to 2.5 seconds instead of about 1.
- Checked: new `tests/unit/tutor-cascade.test.js` (3); 121 of 121 unit tests; `npm run build`; `node --check`; in a browser with a faked server and microphone: the greeting, a spoken turn sent as WAV, captions, board, cost line, the pronunciation note carried into the next turn, talking over the tutor stops its voice, Google never contacted. Not checked from here: real Whisper, Groq and Azure calls (keys only in Vercel). If Azure's voice fails, the answer still shows in the captions and board, silent (logged as `[Tutor] cascade voice`).

## 2026-11-10 — AI tutor, test call: the owner's first real try, and a better brain and ears for it
- The owner's test call (admin account, English B1, Lena, 10.4 minutes, 35 answers), from ai_usage: Azure voice $0.076 (35, median 0.47 s, voice en-US-AmandaMultilingualNeural), Azure checks $0.047 (30), Groq model $0.023 (35, median 0.45 s), Whisper $0.004 (33, median 0.24 s): **$0.15 for 10.4 minutes**. The live engine's last call: about $0.57 per 10 minutes.
- Owner: "sounds dumb, it ruins most things i say even though i said them correctly, and it takes 2 seconds avg to get a reply". From the transcript: the model taught wrong sounds ("visited" with the vowel of "bed"; "favorite" said differently from "favourite" because Whisper wrote the US spelling) and wrong grammar ("He asked if I hadn't visited a museum"), counted points aloud, kept asking for preferences; Whisper made up "Thank you." from silence and heard "Steve" in a sentence; "Have you ever ... gone to Switzerland" was cut in two at a pause (B1 waited 1.2 s, then answered the half).
- Brain (`api/_lib/tutor-cascade.js` `think`): Google's `gemini-3.8-flash` (`TUTOR_CALL_MODEL`) on the existing `GOOGLE_AI_API_KEY`, thinking level low (asked again without it if refused), the written tutor's model as the fallback if Google fails. Its price is $0.75 per million input tokens, $0.075 for input Google has read before in the call, $3.75 output: the instructions are now the same every turn (time left moved to a note at the end, the brief built once per call and kept 15 minutes) so most of each turn is the cheaper part; about $0.002 a turn. New rules: spelling and punctuation are the recogniser's, never British and American spellings as different words; no pronunciation judgements without the speech checker's note; a word that does not fit was probably misheard (ask, never correct it); only rules and sounds it is certain of; no counting points aloud.
- Ears (`api/_lib/tutor-voice.js`): in the call Whisper gets the tutor's last line as a hint of what may be said, and drops pieces it itself doubts were speech (no-speech probability over 0.6 with a low score, a very low score, or repeated text). The written tutor is unchanged.
- Speed: the brief, the conversation and the time left load while Whisper listens. The pause before answering is shorter in the test call (A1/A2 1.1 s, B1 0.9 s, others 0.75 s); if the student goes on talking while an answer is on its way, that answer is dropped and taken out of the conversation (`unheard`), so a split sentence gets one answer to the whole.
- Expected per 10 minutes: about $0.19 (the brain $0.07 instead of $0.02), answers about half a second sooner after the student stops.
- Checked: 4 new tests in `tests/unit/tutor-cascade.test.js` (Whisper filter, time note and cost with the cached part, Gemini asked again without thinking); 125 of 125 unit tests; `npm run build`; `node --check`; the call in a browser with a faked server and microphone (hint sent, an answer talked over is dropped and the next turn says so). Not checked from here: a real Gemini, Whisper and Azure call.

## 2026-11-10 — AI tutor, test call: answers streamed, so the student is answered
- Owner: "i was talking and he basically haven't answered me at all". From ai_usage (session 930f2f4d, Kofi, voice en-US-AlloyTurboMultilingualNeural): Gemini took 1.7 to 5.7 s per answer (Groq had taken 0.45 s) and Azure 1 to 2 s more, so an answer needed 4 to 7 s; the student spoke again meanwhile, and the morning's rule dropped any answer still on its way when the student spoke, so almost none was heard although nearly every turn got one on the server.
- Streaming (`api/tutor.js` cascade-turn, `api/_lib/tutor-cascade.js` `streamThink`, `sayOf`, `cutAt`, `speakPiece`): the answer comes back as lines of JSON. What was heard first; Gemini's reply is read as it is written (`streamGenerateContent`, "say" asked for as the first key), its first sentence is spoken by Azure as soon as it is complete and sent, then the following sentences in order, then the board and the full text. Gemini is asked for the least thinking it allows (`thinkingLevel: minimal`, then `thinkingBudget: 0`, then nothing). If Google fails before writing, the written tutor's model answers and its reply is spoken in the same way. Voice cost settled once per answer.
- Page (`js/app/data.js` `tutorCascadeStream`, `js/app/tutor-live.js` `cascadeLink`): plays each piece as it arrives; shows "Lena is thinking" (`app_tl_thinking`, English only, test call) while waiting. An answer is dropped only when the student goes on within 1.5 s and before it has started speaking (a sentence still being finished); a later one is kept, unless it arrives while the student is talking, when it is not played over them and their new sentence is answered instead. Talking over a voice that is playing stops it.
- The pause before answering now counts the speech filter's own 240 ms (it had waited about 1.15 s at B1, not 0.9).
- Checked: 3 new tests in `tests/unit/tutor-cascade.test.js` (reading "say" from a partial reply, where to cut, a streamed Gemini reply); 128 of 128 unit tests; `npm run build`; `node --check`; in a browser with a faked stream and microphone: speaking starts with the first piece, "thinking" shown, a slow answer arriving while the student talks is not played and the next turn asks for it to be taken out. Not checked from here: real Gemini, Whisper and Azure, and whether Vercel passes the stream on piece by piece (if it holds it back, the call still works, without the head start).

## 2026-11-10 — Classroom and board redesign

Owner: phones optimised; the top of the classroom and the board redesigned for teachers and students; the video animated from small to large; a notice for every new message and every person who joins; the notes easier to reach for the teacher; more material for the board.
- `js/app/classroom.js` (new, loaded by `app/index.html` and `app/teach.html`): one classroom for both dashboards. A slim top bar (leave, class and lesson, the teacher's Board and Notes), the video and a side panel with tabs (Chat, Notes for the teacher, Words). The dashboard sidebar and header are hidden during class. The video moves smoothly (FLIP) between small beside the board and large; on phones the video sits on top, the board under it and the panel is a sheet opened from a Chat button. Notices (toast plus a quiet chime): someone joined or left, a new message from someone else; unread messages show on the Chat tab and button. Messages arrive at once (a Daily app message tells everyone to fetch) and every 6 seconds otherwise. Students now have the class chat in the classroom too.

- Teacher notes in class: the notes fields (`notesFields` in `teach-classes.js`, shared with the notes page) in the Notes tab, saved as a draft while typing; publishing stays on the notes page (link opens a new tab, so the call keeps running).
- Board (`js/app/board.js`): the teacher's row of page buttons replaced by a page switcher (previous, the page's name and number with a menu, next); class controls (answers, who can write, everyone solves this) as one row; a floating icon dock for the tools (pen, highlighter, text, eraser, colours, undo, clear with a second tap). Phones: the dock is a row above the page, a zoom button doubles the page and a move tool pans it. The menu adds pages: six empty pages (lined, squared, two columns, word map, timeline, table), any board page from the other lessons of the course, and the words from the last class. Added pages are in `S.extra`, sent to students with the board and saved in `session_boards.extra` (`supabase/migrations/202611101200_session_boards_extra.sql`, applied and verified).

## 2026-11-12 — Admin console: redesign, live feed, and fixes from an audit against production
- Owner: "improve admin panel ... see what works and what doesn't, improve design and make it a corporate grade panel to see everything that happens inside the website. for example, the matching section. but also everything else. go"
- Audit (every admin query, embed, RPC, row rule and `/api` action checked against the live database): nothing in the schema is broken. Real problems found: "Paying" counted Stripe subscriptions only (0) while purchases now live in `entitlements`; the waiting count ignored the waiting-list plan and room requests; the Students plan list lacked `app`, `open`, `pack` and `exam`, so saving such a student's file would have silently set them to Free; `daily:join-session`, `db:tutor-purge` and `vercel:cron-reminders` were unnamed; `cron-billing` and `cron-rooms` showed as late although they were only added at 04:38 today, after their run times.
- Design: `css/admin.css` (scoped to `body.adm`, only on `/app/admin`): compact forest sidebar with counts beside Teachers, Waiting list and System; a white top bar with section path, a search across students and teachers by name or email (press `/`), a live or sample marker and Refresh; Satoshi headings instead of large condensed capitals, one panel style, smaller buttons and fields, table-like rows; the drawings are left to the learners' dashboards; the panels' entrance stagger is capped at a quarter of a second (the Matching page used to wait over a second for its last panels).
- Overview rebuilt as the console's front page (`js/app/admin.js`, `adminConsole()` in `js/app/data.js`, read-only through the existing admin row rules): eight figures with 30-day trend lines (students, active this week, paying, paid in 30 days, tutor minutes with the admin's own left out, open classes, waiting, AI spend); a live feed of the last 14 days, filterable (people, money, learning, tutor, messages, visits, problems) and built from accounts, applications, news sign-ups, the welcome poll, purchases and failed payments, tutor sessions and added minutes, enrolments, waiting list and offers, class attendance, homework, 1 on 1 bookings, messages, dashboard visits, withdrawal requests, failed AI calls, failed jobs, unprocessed Stripe events and blocked abuse; "Needs you"; today's classes; system health; daily active people and new accounts; who used the tutor; latest student accounts with when they were last seen. Each source fails on its own and is named under "Needs you" if it cannot be read.
- Students, Teachers and Classes lists are tables with column heads (a row opens the record). Students' "Paying" filter and payment column count `entitlements` purchases beside Stripe subscriptions (admin grants and trials do not count).
- No database change. Checked: `npm run build`, `node --check` on the changed scripts and `api/`, every admin section in sample mode with no console errors, the real-data path with stubbed admin responses (database text stays escaped), phone width with no sideways scroll.
- For the owner: the Daily room refusal on 7 Nov ("max_participants cannot be set to that value with your current plan", `daily:join-session`) and the 6 Nov `cron-create-rooms` failure look like the same cause and need the Daily plan or the room size changed; `admin_matching()` reads `room_availability` (empty) rather than `teacher_availability` (12 rows), so Matching shows no teacher times yet; two rating triggers on `teacher_reviews` recalculate ratings twice. Black (forest) blocks are gone from the admin's working views.

## 2026-11-13 — Verified classes and automations that run alone (docs/OPERATIONS_PLAN.md, part 1)
- Owner: "build a system to verify class started, ended, at what time, how many people ... advanced analytics ... we need control, and we need automations that are safe enough to run alone". Owner's answers: held = teacher in the room 90% of the class and at least one learner; a late teacher (5 minutes) is told to the owner, the teacher and the learners; payouts count verified held classes only; a digest every morning. Also asked for screenshots during classes, watching a call and recording (not built yet: see "For the owner").
- Found: class dates became "completed" by the clock (28 so far, only 2 with any learner asking to join); attendance meant asking for a room pass; payouts counted those dates. Daily refused the room size of 40 on 7 Nov.
- Database (`supabase/migrations/202611131000_class_verification_automations.sql`, applied and verified): on `sessions` the actual start and end, teacher's first join and time in the slot, minutes late, learners present, most people at once, `outcome` and `verified_at`; `class_presence` (each person's time per class date), `video_meetings` (Daily's records as sent), `automations` (switch, dry run, daily cap, failures, pause) and `automation_log` (every action, one successful action per key). Admins read; only the server writes.
- Server: `api/_lib/class-verify.js` (Daily's meetings and participants, matched to accounts by the id the meeting token carries; the outcome rule), `api/_lib/automation.js` (switch, dry run, once only, daily cap, log, pause after 3 failures and email the owner), `api/ops.js` with crons every 5 minutes (`cron-class-watch`: teacher-late, class outcomes) and 06:43 UTC (`cron-digest`), and admin actions (switch an automation, read a class date's video room again, send the digest now). Classes taught by the owner's test accounts are verified but never alerted. Teacher-late emails and dashboard notes in eight languages (`class_late` in `api/_lib/email-text/*.json`). Rooms no longer ask Daily for 40 places (`join-session`, `cron-create-rooms`, `create-cohort`).
- Automations at launch: class outcomes on and live (writes Alimun's own records only); morning digest on and live (emails the owner only); teacher-late alert on but in dry run, writing down what it would send, until the owner switches it to live in Admin, Automations.
- Admin: Class log (every class date with a bar of the slot, anyone in the room and the teacher; learners, minutes late, outcome; totals; each teacher's record) and a class date's page (each person on a timeline, joins, Daily's records, what the automations did, "Read the video room again"); Automations (switches, dry run, cap, today's counts, every action); Payouts count classes verified as held and show the unverified and not-held dates beside.
- Checked: 224 of 224 unit tests (9 new: outcomes, rejoins, settling, room names, the automation guard), build, `node --check`, every admin section in sample mode.
- For the owner: switch the teacher-late alert to live after a few correct dry runs; past class dates before 13 Nov have no video record, so they stay "not verified". Screenshots, watching and recording: see the options sent on 13 Nov (Daily's cloud recording and its cost on the current plan; telling learners and teachers in the terms and in the room before any of it).

## 2026-11-13 — Class recordings: proof clips, admin-only recording, deletion, guard
- Owner: card added to Daily (pay as you go); asked what is best for screenshots and recording and the cost; "do not allow teacher to record freely, it would be bad for privacy. only admins and me."
- Chosen (owner agreed to the recommendation): five 60-second proof clips per group class instead of screenshots (about $0.08 a class at Daily's $0.01349 a recorded minute plus $0.003 storage), full recording only by admins when a concern is raised, no recording of every class in full.
- Database (`supabase/migrations/202611131400_class_recordings.sql`, applied and verified): `class_recordings` (proof, full, unknown; Daily's recording id, deletion date, keep), `recording_views` (who watched what, when); automations `proof-clips` (on, dry run), `recording-cleanup` and `recording-guard` (on, live).
- Server: `api/_lib/recordings.js` (start with a maximum length so a clip stops by itself, stop, list, a 15-minute link, delete, pairing Daily's recordings with Alimun's requests); `api/ops.js`: `cron-clips` every minute, pairing and the guard in the 5-minute watch, hourly clean-up; admin actions `record-start`, `record-stop`, `recording-link` (logged as a viewing), `recording-keep`. Clips are skipped while an admin's full recording runs (Daily records one at a time per room).
- Rooms: class rooms allow cloud recording (for the server); one-to-one rooms do not. Passes: teachers are no longer room owners (they get the right to manage participants), admins are; nobody's pass shows the record button. The board recognises the teacher by that right (`js/app/board.js`).
- Admin: a class date's page has Record the class / Stop recording (admins only, while the class is on, with a confirmation), and Recordings (each clip or recording, when, length, deletion date, who watched, Watch, Keep).
- Classroom: a short notice under a group class's title in nine languages (`app_cr_rec_note`). Draft wording for the Terms and Privacy Policy in `docs/RECORDING_NOTICE_DRAFT.md`, not published.
- Checked: 226 of 226 unit tests (clip timing, pairing recordings), build, `node --check`, admin and classroom in sample mode.
- For the owner: approve or change `docs/RECORDING_NOTICE_DRAFT.md`, then switch Proof clips to live in Admin, Automations; a lawyer should read the wording; say whether any learners are under 16.
